### Week 6

#### Monday: Ngā mahi toi — Art, music, and creativity

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 2.5: Expressive vocabulary · AO 2.3: Creative expression · AO 1.4: Cultural arts*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.5 — Arts & creative vocabulary; AO 1.4 — Cultural arts connection

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — The arts in te reo Māori**

Art, music, dance, and craft are central to te ao Māori — they are not extras but essentials. Today we build vocabulary for the arts and creative expression so you can talk about what you make, perform, and love in te reo Māori.

> **[toi](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=toi)** — art / creativity / expertise
> **toi [whakairo](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=whakairo)** — carving (whakairo = to carve / engrave)
> **toi [raranga](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=raranga)** — weaving (raranga = to weave)
> **toi [tā moko](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=t%C4%81+moko)** — tā moko / traditional tattoo
> **toi [puoro](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=puoro)** — music (puoro = music / musical instrument)
> **toi [kori](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=kori)** — dance / movement arts (kori = movement)
> **toi [ataata](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=ataata)** — film / visual arts (ataata = shadow / film)
> **haka** — haka (posture dance / ceremonial challenge)
> **waiata** — song (also: to sing)
> **[poi](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=poi)** — poi (ball on string, used in performance)

Arts vocabulary in sentences:

**Ka whakairo ia i ngā mahi toi.**
She carves artworks.

**He tohunga raranga tōna māmā.**
Her mother is an expert weaver. (tohunga = expert)

**Kei te ako au i te haka.**
I am learning haka.

**Ko te waiata tōku mahi toi pai rawa atu.**
Singing is my absolute favourite art form.

**Ka kaoa e ia ngā poi.**
She swings the poi. (kaoa = swing / wave)

New words:
**tohunga** — expert / skilled person / specialist
**mahi toi** — artwork / creative work
**ahua** — image / appearance / likeness
**kaoa** — swing / wave / rotate

*Tip: Toi means both "art" and "expertise" in te reo Māori. Tohunga (expert) comes from the same root. In te ao Māori, art and skill are the same thing — to make something beautiful is to demonstrate mastery.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Arts vocabulary**

Use arts vocabulary in sentences about real creative activities.

- Name your favourite art form: Ko tōku mahi toi pai rawa atu ko [art form] nō te mea…
- Describe a skill: He tohunga [art form] tōku [family member/friend].
- Past tense: what did you make or perform? I [verb] au i [art form/object].
- Future intention: Kei te hiahia au ki te ako i te [art form]. Māku e [action].

_Discussion prompt: Toi means both art AND expertise. In English, we separate these — "art" and "skill" feel like different things. In te ao Māori, they are the same word. What does this tell us about how Māori understood creative practice? Can you think of examples where art and skill really ARE inseparable?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Arts sentences**

Write about your creative life using arts vocabulary.

- Write three sentences about arts activities using three different toi words.
- Write a He tohunga sentence about someone you know who is skilled in an art form.
- Write a past tense sentence about something you made or performed.
- ✦ Bonus: Write a paragraph (7 sentences) about a creative project you have done or would like to do. Include: the art form, who it involved (pronouns), what you did (Ka sequence), how it felt (emotion vocabulary), and what it meant (He mea nui/taonga tēnei nō te mea…).

#### Tuesday: He kōrero mō te haka — The language of haka

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 1.4: Cultural identity · AO 2.5: Expressive vocabulary · AO 2.3: Extended cultural explanation*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 1.4 — Haka & cultural identity; AO 2.5 — Haka vocabulary

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Understanding and talking about haka**

Haka is one of the most recognised Māori art forms in the world — but it is often misunderstood. Today we learn how to TALK about haka in te reo Māori, what it means, and when it is used.

> **Ko te [haka](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=haka) he mahi toi nō te reo Māori.**
> Haka is an art form of te reo Māori.
> 
> **He haka mō ngā wā katoa — mō te hari, mō te pōuri, mō te [whakataetae](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=whakataetae).**
> There is a haka for every occasion — for joy, for sadness, for competition.
> 
> **Ko "Ka Mate" tētahi haka rongonui o Aotearoa.**
> "Ka Mate" is a famous haka of New Zealand.
> 
> **He [tohu](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=tohu) [aroha](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=aroha) te haka — he tohu [kaha](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=kaha) hoki.**
> Haka is a sign of love — it is also a sign of strength.

Haka vocabulary:
**rongonui** — famous / well-known
**whakataetae** — competition
**tohu** — sign / symbol / marker
**kaha** — strength / powerful (also: hard/strongly as adverb)
**ngā wā katoa** — every occasion / all times
**He haka mō te tangi.** — A haka for a funeral/mourning.
**He haka mō te pōhiri.** — A haka for a welcome.
**He haka mō te whakataetae.** — A haka for competition.

Parts of haka:
**kūaha** — opening
**whakakapi** — conclusion
**reo** — voice / words
**ātea** — space / arena
**ngākau** — heart / spirit (must be in the performance)

*Tip: He tohu aroha te haka — haka is a sign of love. This surprises many people who see only the fierce exterior. But the fierce face and the deep love are not opposites in te ao Māori — they are two expressions of the same passion.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Haka conversation**

Discuss haka using today's vocabulary.

- Define haka: Ko te tikanga o te haka ko te [explanation]. Use Ko te tikanga structure from last week.
- Give occasions: He haka mō te [occasion] — list three occasions when haka is performed.
- Express what haka means: He tohu [word] te haka. He tohu [word] hoki.
- Your experience: Kei te hiahia au ki te ako i te haka / I ako au i te haka i [time].

_Discussion prompt: He tohu aroha te haka — haka is a sign of love — he tohu kaha hoki — and a sign of strength. How can love and strength be expressed in the same action? Can you think of other examples in life where love and strength are not opposites but one and the same?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Haka explanation**

Write an explanation of haka in te reo Māori — for someone who does not know what it is.

- Define haka: Ko te tikanga o te haka ko te…
- Give three occasions: He haka mō te [X], he haka mō te [Y], he haka mō te [Z].
- Explain the meaning: He tohu [noun] te haka nō te mea…
- ✦ Bonus: Write a full explanation of haka (8 sentences) that could go in a school brochure or wall display. Cover: what it is, when it is done, what it means, why it matters to Māori culture, and what it means to you personally.

#### Wednesday: Hanga rerenga — Creative and cultural writing

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 2.5: Creative expression · AO 1.4: Cultural identity · AO 2.3: Extended creative writing*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.5 — Creative writing; AO 1.4 — Cultural expression

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Writing that is alive with culture**

The most powerful te reo writing does two things at once — it says something in te reo AND it says something through te reo, using the language itself as a window into te ao Māori. Today we write with that double purpose.

> An example of culturally rich writing:
> 
> **Ko te haka ko tōku reo — he reo ō-roto, he reo ō-waho.**
> Haka is my language — an inward language, an outward language.
> 
> **Ka ara ōku ringa ki runga — ko tērā he [mihi](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=mihi) ki ngā [tīpuna](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=t%C4%ABpuna).**
> My arms rise — that is a greeting to the ancestors.
> 
> **Ka kōrero ōku waewae — ka tangi ōku karu.**
> My feet speak — my eyes cry.
> 
> **Ko te haka tōku ara ki a rātou — ki ngā tīpuna, ki te ao [mārama](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=m%C4%81rama).**
> Haka is my path to them — to the ancestors, to the world of light.

Techniques for culturally rich writing:

1. **Use body + action to carry cultural meaning**:
"Ka ara ōku ringa ki runga" — arms rising as a greeting

2. **Personify natural/cultural elements**:
"Ka kōrero ōku waewae" — feet speak

3. **Connect personal action to ancestral meaning**:
Ko tērā he mihi ki ngā tīpuna — that is a greeting to the ancestors

4. **Use Ko to make identity statements about art**:
"Ko te haka tōku ara ki a rātou"

New phrases:
**he reo ō-roto** — an inner language
**he reo ō-waho** — an outer language
**ao mārama** — world of light (the realm of the living or of enlightenment)

*Tip: Ko te haka tōku ara ki a rātou — ki ngā tīpuna. The art form is a pathway. What art form is YOUR pathway — to understanding, to connection, to something beyond words? Can you say that in te reo Māori?*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Culturally rich writing**

Write a passage that uses an art form as a window into identity.

- Start with a Ko statement about the art: Ko te [art form] ko tōku [what it is to you].
- Describe a physical action: Ka [body action] ōku [body part] — ko tērā he [meaning].
- Connect to the ancestors or community: Ko tērā tōku ara ki [who/where].
- Close with a He identity statement: He [quality] te [art form] — he [quality] hoki.

_Discussion prompt: Ko te haka ko tōku reo — haka is my language. What does it mean to say an art form IS your language? What can haka say that words cannot? Is there an art form that speaks for you when words fail?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Creative cultural writing**

Write a passage about an art form that matters to you.

- Write a Ko identity statement: Ko te [art form] ko tōku [meaning to you].
- Write two body-action sentences that carry cultural meaning.
- Connect to something beyond yourself — ancestors, community, nature.
- ✦ Bonus: Write a full piece (8 sentences) about an art form that matters to you. Use: Ko for identity, body vocabulary for action, emotional vocabulary, cultural connection, personification, and close with Ko tērā tōku ara ki…

#### Thursday: Kōrero — He kōrero mō ngā mahi toi — Arts conversation

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 2.5: Arts vocabulary · AO 1.4: Cultural arts · AO 2.3: Extended conversation*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.5 — Arts conversation; AO 1.4 — Arts & identity

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — A conversation about creative life**

Today we have a full conversation about art, music, and creativity — drawing on everything from this week and the whole term.

> **A:** Ko ēhea ngā mahi toi e hiahia ana koe ki te ako?
> **B:** Kei te hiahia au ki te ako i te haka me te waiata — he mahi toi ātaahua ērā ki a au.
> **A:** He aha tōu mahi toi ināianei?
> **B:** Kei te ako au i te raranga. He mea uaua engari he tino ātaahua ngā [hua](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=hua)!
> **A:** He [tohunga](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=tohunga) koe?
> **B:** Kāore anō — he ākonga noa iho au ināianei! Engari āpōpō, ka taea e au te [raranga](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=raranga) i ngā āhua rerekē.
> **A:** Ka pai tāu māia! Ko ēhea ngā āhuatanga pai rawa atu mōu mō tēnei mahi toi?
> **B:** Ko ēnei: he mārie te noho me te raranga, ka mārama hoki tōku ngākau i ngā āhua ka [puta](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=puta) mai.

New conversation vocabulary:
**Ko ēhea ngā mahi toi…?** — Which art forms…?
**he mahi toi ātaahua ērā ki a au** — those are beautiful art forms to me
**ngā hua** — the results / the outcomes
**he ākonga noa iho au** — I am just a learner (noa = just/merely, iho = downward particle, emphasising humility)
**āhua rerekē** — different forms / various designs
**āhuatanga pai rawa atu** — absolute best aspects / features
**ka puta mai** — that emerge / that come forth (puta = emerge)
**ka mārama tōku ngākau** — my heart becomes clear/enlightened

*Tip: He ākonga noa iho au — I am just a learner. The noa iho shows humility — making yourself small in a gracious way. In te ao Māori, calling yourself just a learner is not weakness — it is the correct relationship to knowledge.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Arts conversation**

Have a full conversation about your creative life in te reo Māori.

- I ask: Ko ēhea ngā mahi toi e hiahia ana koe ki te ako? Answer with your arts interests.
- I ask: He aha tōu mahi toi ināianei? Answer with what you do now.
- I ask: He tohunga koe? Answer humbly — He ākonga noa iho au / Kāore anō.
- I ask: Ko ēhea ngā āhuatanga pai rawa atu mōu? Answer with Ko ēnei: a list of what you love about it.

_Discussion prompt: He ākonga noa iho au — I am just a learner. And ka mārama tōku ngākau i ngā āhua ka puta mai — my heart becomes clear at the forms that emerge. What does it mean for your heart to become clear through making something? Have you ever felt that? What were you doing?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Arts identity**

Write about your creative life and what it means to you.

- Write which art forms you love: Ko ēnei ōku mahi toi: he [X], he [Y], he [Z].
- Write your current skill level: He ākonga noa iho au / He āhua tohunga au i te [art form].
- Write what you love about your favourite art form: Ko ēnei ngā āhuatanga pai rawa atu: he…
- ✦ Bonus: Write a full arts identity paragraph (8 sentences). Include: which arts you love, your current level, what you love about it, one Ko statement about what the art means to you, a Ka taea sentence about what you can do, and a moemoeā about where you want to go.

#### Friday: Aromatawai — Quiz & Review


**Monday questions**

- What does "tohunga" mean?
  - student / learner 
  - expert / skilled person ✅
  - teacher 
  - artist 
- "Ko tōku mahi toi pai rawa atu ko te waiata." What does waiata mean?
  - carving 
  - weaving 
  - song / singing ✅
  - poi 

**Tuesday questions**

- What does "rongonui" mean?
  - sacred 
  - famous / well-known ✅
  - powerful 
  - ancient 
- "He tohu aroha te haka — he tohu kaha hoki." What two things is haka a sign of?
  - anger and power 
  - sadness and strength 
  - love and strength ✅
  - tradition and sport 

**Wednesday questions**

- "Ko te haka ko tōku reo." What does this Ko statement express?
  - Haka and language are different things 
  - Haka is the speaker's language — their way of expressing themselves ✅
  - Te reo Māori is a form of haka 
  - The speaker cannot speak te reo 
- What is "ao mārama"?
  - a dark night 
  - the world of light / realm of enlightenment ✅
  - the ocean 
  - a rainy day 

**Thursday questions**

- "He ākonga noa iho au." What does this expression convey?
  - I am an expert 
  - I am refusing to learn 
  - I am just a learner — humble acknowledgement of learning status ✅
  - I do not like learning 
- "Ka mārama tōku ngākau i ngā āhua ka puta mai." What does "ka puta mai" mean?
  - that disappear 
  - that are ancient 
  - that emerge / that come forth ✅
  - that are difficult

### Week 7

#### Monday: He kōrero mō te taiao — Environment and kaitiakitanga

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 1.4: Connection to land · AO 2.4: Natural world · AO 2.3: Extended environmental language*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 1.4 — Kaitiakitanga & environment; AO 2.4 — Environmental vocabulary

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Guardianship of the natural world**

Kaitiakitanga — guardianship of the natural environment — is one of the most important concepts in te ao Māori and in modern New Zealand. Today we build the vocabulary to talk about the environment, what threatens it, and what guardianship means in practice.

> **[kaitiakitanga](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=kaitiakitanga)** — environmental guardianship / stewardship / stewardship
> **[kaitiaki](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=kaitiaki)** — guardian / protector (kai- + tiaki) / protector (kai- + tiaki)
> **taiao** — natural world / environment
> **[papatūānuku](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=papat%C5%AB%C4%81nuku)** — the earth / ground (Papa = earth mother) / ground (Papa = earth mother)
> **[tangaroa](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=tangaroa)** — the sea / Tangaroa (atua of the sea) / Tangaroa (atua of the sea)
> **[tāne](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=t%C4%81ne)** — the forest / Tāne (atua of the forest and birds) / Tāne (atua of the forest and birds)
> **[toitū](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=toit%C5%AB)** — sustainable / enduring / intact / enduring / intact
> **[paru](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=paru)** — polluted / dirty / muddy / dirty / muddy
> **[mauri](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=mauri)** — life force / vitality / vitality
> **[ora](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=ora)** — well / alive / thriving / thriving

Environmental language in sentences:

**Ko tātou ngā kaitiaki o tēnei taiao.**
We are the guardians of this natural world.

**Kāore e toitū ana te ao ināianei — he paru ngā awa, he ngaro ngā manu.**
The world is not sustainable right now — the rivers are polluted, the birds are disappearing.

**Me tiaki tātou i te mauri o ngā awa, o ngā ngahere, o ngā maunga.**
We must protect the life force of the rivers, forests, and mountains.

**Ka ora te [taiao](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=taiao), ka ora tātou.**
When the environment thrives, we thrive.

New words:
**ngaro** — lost / disappeared / extinct
**tiakina** — be protected (passive of tiaki)
**toitū te taiao** — a sustainable/enduring environment

*Tip: Ka ora te taiao, ka ora tātou — when the environment is well, we are well. This sentence sums up kaitiakitanga perfectly. The earth's wellbeing and ours are not separate. Learn this sentence cold.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Environmental language**

Use environmental vocabulary in sentences about the real world.

- State our responsibility: **Ko tātou ngā kaitiaki o tēnei taiao.** Add a reason: nō te mea…
- Name a problem: **Kāore e toitū ana te [environmental feature] ināianei nō te mea…**
- Use me for responsibility: **Me tiaki tātou i te mauri o [feature].**
- Close with the core principle: **Ka ora te taiao, ka ora tātou.** Discuss what this means.

_Discussion prompt: Ka ora te taiao, ka ora tātou — the wellbeing of the environment is the wellbeing of people. In English we often talk about "protecting the environment" as if it is separate from us. How does the te reo sentence change that framing? What does it say about the relationship between people and the natural world?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Kaitiakitanga writing**

Write about environmental guardianship in te reo Māori.

- Write your responsibility statement: Ko au tētahi kaitiaki o te taiao nō te mea…
- Name one environmental issue using Kāore e toitū ana… nō te mea…
- Write what you will do: Māku e [action] hei tiaki i [environmental feature].
- ✦ Bonus: Write a kaitiakitanga statement (8 sentences) — what kaitiakitanga means, what the problems are, what must be done (me sentences), what you personally commit to (māku e), and close with Ka ora te taiao, ka ora tātou.

#### Tuesday: Ngā kupu o te ture — Rights, rules, and responsibilities

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 2.3: Extended communication · AO 1.6: Social conventions · Grammar Level 1–2: Me, kaua, tikanga in social contexts*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.3 — Rights & responsibilities; AO 1.6 — Social conventions & rules

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Rights, rules, and what we must do**

Language for rights and responsibilities is important for full participation in society. Today we build the vocabulary for rules, what is allowed, what must be done, and what we have the right to.

> **he [tikanga](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=tikanga)** — a rule / a correct practice / a right
> **He tikanga tōku ki te kōrero.**
> It is my right to speak.
> 
> **Me mahi tātou i tēnei.** — We must do this.
> **[Kaua](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=Kaua) e mahi i tēnā.** — Do not do that. (kaua = do not — negative command)
> **E [āhei](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=%C4%81hei) ana koe ki te…** — You are permitted/able to…
> **Kāore e āhei ana koe ki te…** — You are not permitted to…
> 
> **He aha ngā tikanga o tō mātou kura?**
> What are the rules of our school?

Rights and rules vocabulary:
**āhei** — permitted / allowed / able to
**kaua e [verb]** — do not [verb] (negative command)
**kaua e tū ake** — do not stand up
**kaua e kōrero kino** — do not speak badly
**he tikanga** — a rule / custom / right
**He tikanga tōku ki te [verb].** — It is my right to [verb].
**He kawenga ōku ki te [verb].** — It is my responsibility to [verb].
**tōpū** — group / together (tōpū mahi = working group)
**whakaaro nui** — consideration / deep thought

For rules:
**Me [verb] tātou** — We must [verb] (rule/obligation)
**Kaua e [verb]** — Do not [verb] (prohibition)
**E āhei ana koe ki te [verb]** — You are allowed to [verb]

*Tip: Kaua e [verb] = do not [verb]. This is the negative command form. More polite than a bare command but still clear. Kaua e waiho i tō para ki reira! — Don't leave your rubbish there!*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — School rules in te reo**

Express your school rules in te reo Māori using me, kaua, and āhei.

- A school rule: **Me whakarongo mai ngā ākonga ki tō rātou kaiako.** (Students must listen to their teacher.)
- A prohibition: **Kaua e omaoma i roto i ngā whare katoa.** (Do not run inside the buildings.)
- A right: **He tikanga ō ngā ākonga katoa ki te pātai.** (All students have the right to ask questions.)
- A responsibility: **He kawenga ō ngā ākonga ki te tiaki i tō rātou kura.**

_Discussion prompt: He tikanga tōku ki te kōrero — it is my right to speak. This sentence connects to Article 13 of the UN Convention on the Rights of the Child. Te reo Māori has the vocabulary to talk about rights and justice. Why does a language need this vocabulary? What happens when a language cannot talk about rights?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Rights and rules**

Write your own school or community rules in te reo Māori.

- Write three school rules using me [verb] structure.
- Write two prohibitions using kaua e [verb].
- Write one right and one responsibility using He tikanga/kawenga…
- ✦ Bonus: Write a "Student Bill of Rights" in te reo (8 sentences) — three rights (He tikanga ōku ki te…), three responsibilities (He kawenga ōku ki te…), and two prohibitions (Kaua e…). This could go on a classroom wall.

#### Wednesday: Hanga rerenga — Argument and persuasion extended

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 2.3: Extended persuasive writing · Grammar Level 1–2: Full argument toolkit in te reo*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.3 — Persuasive writing; AO 2.5 — Formal argument vocabulary

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Making a case in te reo Māori**

You have learned to argue, compare, and use linking words. Today we assemble the full argument toolkit into a persuasive piece — the kind of writing that could appear in a school speech, a debate, or a submission.

> **Tōku [tūāhuatanga](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=t%C5%AB%C4%81huatanga):** He nui ake ngā hua o te kaitiakitanga i ngā uauatanga.
> <i>My position: The benefits of environmental guardianship are greater than the difficulties.</i>
> 
> **Tāku taunakitanga tuatahi:** Ka ora te taiao, ka ora tātou — ko tēnā he tauira o te pono.
> <i>My first evidence: When the environment thrives, we thrive — that is an example of truth.</i>
> 
> **Waihoki:** He tikanga ōu mō āpōpō ō ngā uri — me [tiaki](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=tiaki) tātou ināianei.
> <i>Moreover: It is your descendants' right for tomorrow — we must protect now.</i>
> 
> **Nō reira:** me mahi tātou katoa hei [kaitiaki](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=kaitiaki) o tēnei taiao.
> <i>Therefore: we must all work as guardians of this environment.</i>

Full argument structure:

1. **Tōku tūāhuatanga:** [position] — My position is…
2. **Tāku taunakitanga tuatahi / tuarua:** [evidence] — My first/second evidence
3. **Waihoki:** [additional point] — Moreover
4. **Heoi anō:** [concession to other side]
5. **Nō reira:** [conclusion] — Therefore

New words:
**tūāhuatanga** — position / characteristic / quality
**[taunakitanga](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=taunakitanga)** — evidence (from last week!) / support / argument
**tuatahi / tuarua / tuatoru** — first / second / third
**pono** — true / honest / truth
**uri** — descendants (you know this word now!)

*Tip: Tāku taunakitanga tuatahi / tuarua — my first/second evidence. Taunakitanga is the key word for evidence or supporting argument. Learn it — it makes your argument sound like real persuasive writing.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Build the argument**

Together we build a persuasive argument about kaitiakitanga using the full structure.

- State position: **Tōku tūāhuatanga: me tiaki tātou i te taiao o Aotearoa nō te mea…**
- First evidence: **Tāku taunakitanga tuatahi: Ka ora te taiao, ka ora tātou.**
- Second evidence with waihoki: **Waihoki, he tikanga ōu mō āpōpō ō ngā uri.**
- Conclusion with nō reira: **Nō reira, me mahi tātou katoa hei kaitiaki.**

_Discussion prompt: Tāku taunakitanga — my evidence. Good arguments don't just state opinions — they provide evidence. In te ao Māori, evidence often comes from proverbs (whakataukī), from the natural world, and from the actions of ancestors. What makes evidence convincing in te ao Māori? Is it different from what convinces in a Western argument?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Your argument**

Write a persuasive argument in te reo Māori about a topic you care about.

- State your position: Tōku tūāhuatanga: [position].
- Give two pieces of evidence: Tāku taunakitanga tuatahi / tuarua.
- Add waihoki (moreover) and make a third point.
- ✦ Bonus: Write a full persuasive piece (8 sentences) — position, three evidence points (tuatahi/tuarua/tuatoru), a waihoki point, a concession with heoi anō, and a nō reira conclusion. Choose a topic you care about.

#### Thursday: Kōrero — He kōrero mō Aotearoa — Talking about New Zealand

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 1.4: Connection to land · AO 2.4: Natural world · AO 2.3: Extended conversation*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 1.4 — Aotearoa identity; AO 2.4 — NZ environment conversation

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — What is Aotearoa?**

Today we have a conversation about Aotearoa — what it is, what it means, and what our relationship to it is. This is identity conversation at the national level — connecting language, land, and belonging.

> **A:** He aha tō whakaaro mō Aotearoa?
> **B:** Ko Aotearoa tōku kāinga — ko ia tōku wāhi tupu, tōku wāhi tiaki.
> **A:** He aha ngā āhuatanga pai rawa atu o Aotearoa ki a koe?
> **B:** Ko ēnei: he ātaahua te taiao, he [māia](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=m%C4%81ia) ngā tāngata, he rangatira hoki te reo Māori o tēnei [whenua](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=whenua).
> **A:** He aha ōu mōrearea mō Aotearoa?
> **B:** Ko tōku [mōrearea](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=m%C5%8Drearea): ka [ngaro](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=ngaro) ngā manu o ēnei [motu](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=motu), ka ngaro hoki te reo — me tiaki tātou i ērā taonga.
> **A:** He aha tō kawenga ki tēnei whenua?
> **B:** He [kawenga](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=kawenga) ōku ki te ako i te reo Māori, ki te tiaki i te taiao, ā ki te whakaaro nui mō ngā [uri](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=uri).

New Aotearoa vocabulary:
**wāhi tupu** — place of growth / where you grew up
**mōrearea** — concern / worry / risk
**motu** — island / islands (Aotearoa is a motu)
**taonga** — treasure (you know this well now)
**He aha ōu mōrearea?** — What are your concerns?
**he rangatira hoki te reo Māori** — te reo Māori is also a chiefly language
**kawenga ki tēnei whenua** — responsibility to this land
**uri** — descendants (used here for future generations)

*Tip: Ko Aotearoa tōku kāinga — ko ia tōku wāhi tupu, tōku wāhi tiaki. Three Ko statements in a row, connected by commas. This creates a feeling of layering — adding depth to what home means. Use layered Ko statements when you want to say something has many dimensions.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Aotearoa conversation**

Build a conversation about Aotearoa, its beauty, and your responsibilities to it.

- I ask: He aha tō whakaaro mō Aotearoa? Answer with Ko Aotearoa tōku kāinga — ko ia…
- I ask: He aha ngā āhuatanga pai rawa atu o Aotearoa ki a koe? Answer with Ko ēnei list.
- I ask: He aha ōu mōrearea mō Aotearoa? Answer with Ko tōku mōrearea: ka ngaro…
- I ask: He aha tōu kawenga ki tēnei whenua? Answer with He kawenga ōku ki te…

_Discussion prompt: He kawenga ōku ki te ako i te reo Māori, ki te tiaki i te taiao, ā ki te whakaaro nui mō ngā uri. Three responsibilities in one sentence. Of these three — learning the language, protecting the environment, thinking deeply about future generations — which feels most urgent to you? Why?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Tōu whanaungatanga ki Aotearoa**

Write about your relationship with Aotearoa.

- Write three Ko statements about what Aotearoa means to you.
- Write Ko ēnei list of the best things about Aotearoa: Ko ēnei ngā āhuatanga pai rawa atu.
- Write your mōrearea: Ko tōku mōrearea mō Aotearoa ko…
- ✦ Bonus: Write a full statement about your relationship with Aotearoa (8 sentences) — what it is to you, its best features, your concerns, your responsibilities, and what you will do. Close with Ka ora te taiao, ka ora tātou.

- "Kaua e omaoma i roto i ngā whare." What type of sentence is this?

- "E āhei ana koe ki te pātai." What does this sentence tell you?
  - You must ask a question

- "Tāku taunakitanga tuarua." What does tuarua indicate?

### Week 8

#### Monday: Whakataukī — Proverbs and their wisdom

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 2.5: Figurative language · AO 1.4: Cultural wisdom · AO 2.3: Extended explanation*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.5 — Proverbs & figurative language; AO 1.4 — Cultural wisdom

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — The wisdom of whakataukī**

Whakataukī — proverbs — are one of the richest forms of te reo Māori. They carry generations of wisdom in a few words. Today we explore the most important whakataukī for your learning journey, understand their meaning, and learn to use them.

> **He aha te mea nui o te ao?**
> **He tangata, he tangata, he tangata.**
> What is the most important thing in the world? It is people, it is people, it is people.
> 
> **Hutia te [rito](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=rito) o te [harakeke](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=harakeke),**
> **kei hea te [kōmako](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=k%C5%8Dmako) e kō?**
> If you pull the heart of the flax bush, where will the bellbird sing?
> (meaning: if you destroy the centre of the family/community, what happens to its members?)
> 
> **Ehara tāku toa i te toa [takitahi](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=takitahi),**
> **he toa [takitini](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=takitini).**
> My strength is not the strength of one, it is the strength of many.
> 
> **[Whāia](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=Wh%C4%81ia) te iti [kahurangi](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=kahurangi);**
> **ki te [tūohu](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=t%C5%ABōhu) koe, me he maunga [teitei](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=teitei).**
> Pursue excellence; if you must bow your head, let it be to a high mountain.

Understanding whakataukī:

Whakataukī work on two levels:
1. The LITERAL meaning — what the words say
2. The DEEPER meaning — what they teach

"Hutia te rito o te harakeke" — literally about a flax bush and a bellbird. Deeper: the flax bush is the family; the rito (heart shoot) is the child or the core; if you destroy the centre, the community falls apart.

"Whāia te iti kahurangi" — pursue the small precious thing (excellence). If you must bow, bow only to something truly great (a high mountain). Meaning: only humble yourself before things truly worthy of respect.

New words:
**harakeke** — flax plant (native NZ flax)
**rito** — the heart shoot of the flax / the central child
**kōmako** — bellbird
**toa takitahi** — the strength of one alone
**toa takitini** — the strength of many together

*Tip: To explain a whakataukī: first state the literal meaning, then say Ko tā tēnei whakataukī mōhio ko te [deeper meaning]. Always explain BOTH levels.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Whakataukī meanings**

For each whakataukī, we unpack the literal meaning and then the deeper meaning together.

- "He aha te mea nui o te ao? He tangata, he tangata, he tangata." → Literal: what is the most important thing? People, people, people. → Deeper: people are at the centre of everything.
- "Ehara tāku toa i te toa takitahi, he toa takitini." → Literal: my strength is not from one, it is from many. → Deeper: collective strength is greater than individual strength.
- "Whāia te iti kahurangi." → Pursue excellence. When would you use this whakataukī? Who would you say it to?
- Choose one whakataukī and explain it: Ko tā tēnei whakataukī mōhio ko te…

_Discussion prompt: "Hutia te rito o te harakeke, kei hea te kōmako e kō?" The flax family: the outer leaves protect the inner leaves which protect the rito (heart shoot/child). The rito must be protected. This is the architecture of the family in te ao Māori. How does a plant become a model for how families should work?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Whakataukī**

Explore and explain whakataukī in te reo Māori.

- Write the literal meaning of one whakataukī: Ko te tikanga ōpaki o tēnei whakataukī ko te…
- Write the deeper meaning: Ko tā tēnei whakataukī mōhio ko te…
- Write when you would use this whakataukī and who to.
- ✦ Bonus: Choose your favourite whakataukī from today. Write a full explanation (8 sentences) — the words, the literal meaning, the deeper meaning, the context for using it, and why it speaks to you personally. End with how this whakataukī connects to your own life.

#### Tuesday: Ngā kupu hou o te pūtaiao — Science and discovery vocabulary

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 2.5: Vocabulary development · AO 2.3: Extended explanation · Grammar Level 1–2: Science and inquiry vocabulary*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.5 — Science vocabulary; AO 2.3 — Inquiry & explanation

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Exploring and explaining the world**

Te reo Māori has its own words for scientific inquiry — observation, hypothesis, experiment, and discovery. Today we learn this vocabulary so that science learning can happen in te reo as naturally as in English.

> **[pūtaiao](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=p%C5%ABtaiao)** — science / nature study / nature study
> **[matai](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=matai)** — to observe / examine / study / examine / study
> **[whakamātau](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=whakam%C4%81tau)** — experiment / to test / to test
> **rangahau** — research / to research
> **[kitenga](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=kitenga)** — discovery / finding (kite + nga) / finding (kite + nga)
> **pātai** — question / to ask (also: hypothesis)
> **whakaaro** — thought / theory / idea
> **taunakitanga** — evidence (from last week!)
> **hua** — result / outcome
> **[hāngū](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=h%C4%81ng%C5%AB)** — accurate / precise

Science process in te reo:

1. **Ka pātai** mātou i tētahi pātai. — We asked a question.
2. **Ka whakaaro** mātou mō tā mātou whakamātau. — We thought about our experiment.
3. **Ka whakamātau** mātou. — We experimented.
4. **Ka matai** mātou i ngā hua. — We observed the results.
5. **Ka kitea** e mātou tētahi mea hou. — We discovered something new. (kitea = passive of kite)

Explaining findings:
**I kitea e mātou ko te [finding].**
We discovered that [finding].

**He pono tō mātou whakaaro nō te mea…**
Our theory is correct because…

**He rereke ngā hua i ō mātou tūmanako.**
The results were different from our expectations. (tūmanako = expectations/hopes)

*Tip: Ka kitea e mātou — we discovered. Kitea is the passive of kite (see/find). The passive is used here because the discovery "came to us" rather than us actively finding it. This reflects a Māori way of seeing knowledge — it reveals itself to those who are ready.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Science inquiry sequence**

Build a science inquiry description using the Te Reo Māori process vocabulary.

- Start with a question: Ka pātai mātou: **He aha te mea ka hanga ai te kapua?** (What makes a cloud?)
- State the experiment: Ka whakamātau mātou — i mahi mātou i tētahi whakamātau mō te wai.
- State findings: I kitea e mātou ko te [finding]. He [adjective] ngā hua.
- Evaluate: He pono/hē tō mātou whakaaro? (pono = true, hē = wrong/incorrect)

_Discussion prompt: Ka kitea e mātou — we discovered. The passive voice makes the discovery something that came to you, not something you grabbed. In te ao Māori, knowledge is often described as something that reveals itself to a prepared mind. How is this different from the Western idea of "finding" knowledge? Which feels more accurate to how discovery actually happens?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Science recount**

Write a science inquiry recount in te reo Māori.

- Write your question: Ka pātai mātou:…
- Write your experiment: Ka whakamātau mātou — Ka matai mātou i…
- Write your findings: I kitea e mātou ko te…
- ✦ Bonus: Write a full science inquiry recount (8 sentences) — the question, the experiment, the observation, the findings, whether your theory was correct, and what you will explore next. Use Ka for the sequence, I for specific facts, and Kua for completed states.

#### Wednesday: Hanga rerenga — Research and explanation writing

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — AO 2.3: Extended informational writing · AO 2.5: Technical vocabulary · Grammar Level 1–2: Explanation text structures*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.3 — Informational & explanatory writing; AO 2.5 — Technical writing

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Writing to explain and inform**

Today we write in the explanation/information genre — the kind of writing that explains how something works, why something happens, or what something means. This is a different genre from narrative or argument, and it has its own vocabulary and structure.

> **Topic: He aha te āniwaniwa?**
> 
> **Ko te āniwaniwa he tohu ātaahua o te āhua o te rangi.**
> The rainbow is a beautiful sign of the weather.
> 
> **Ka [puta](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=puta) mai te āniwaniwa ina [tutaki](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=tutaki) ana te rā me te ua.**
> The rainbow appears when the sun and rain meet.
> 
> **Ka [wehewehea](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=wehewehea) e te rā ngā tae o te ua — ka puta mai ngā tae katoa.**
> The sun separates the colours of the rain — all the colours emerge.
> 
> **He tohu aroha tēnei i te tikanga Māori — he tohu nō ngā [atua](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=atua).**
> In Māori tradition, this is a sign of love — a sign from the gods.

Explanation text structure:

1. **Topic sentence** — Ko te [X] he [definition/description].
2. **Process** — Ka [verb] ana i [X], ka [result].
3. **Evidence/example** — He tauira: [example].
4. **Cultural/additional meaning** — I te tikanga Māori, ko te tikanga o tēnei ko…

New explanatory vocabulary:
**ina… ana** — when / whenever (conditional time clause)
**tutaki** — meet / encounter
**wehewehe** — separate / divide
**ka puta mai** — emerge / come forth
**he tohu nō ngā atua** — a sign from the gods/spiritual realm
**atua** — god / spiritual being / ancestor deity

*Tip: Ina… ana = whenever / when (that happens). This is your explanation connector — Ka X ina Y ana = X happens when Y happens. It is the te reo equivalent of "when" or "whenever" in an explanation.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Write an explanation**

Together we write an explanation of a natural phenomenon in te reo Māori.

- Topic sentence: Ko te [phenomenon] he [definition]. What natural phenomenon are we explaining?
- Process: Ka puta mai te [X] ina [Y] ana.
- Evidence: He tauira: [specific example].
- Cultural meaning: I te tikanga Māori, ko te tikanga o tēnei ko te…

_Discussion prompt: He tohu nō ngā atua — a sign from the gods. In te ao Māori, natural phenomena are not just physical events — they carry meaning. A rainbow is not just refracted light; it is a sign from the spiritual realm. How does science and spiritual meaning exist together in te ao Māori? Can both be true at the same time?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Explanation writing**

Write an explanation of a natural phenomenon or concept in te reo Māori.

- Write a topic sentence: Ko te [phenomenon] he [definition].
- Write the process using ina… ana: Ka [result] ina [cause] ana.
- Write a cultural meaning: I te tikanga Māori, ko te tikanga o tēnei ko te…
- ✦ Bonus: Write a full explanation (8 sentences) about a natural phenomenon, cultural practice, or concept from this term. Include: definition, process, example, cultural meaning, and your own reflection on what this means to you.

#### Thursday: Kōrero — He kōrero hūnuku — A deep conversation

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — All Term 3 AOs · AO 2.3: Extended sophisticated conversation*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.3 — Extended sophisticated conversation; AO 1.4 — Identity & culture; AO 2.5 — Sophisticated vocabulary

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — A conversation about what matters**

This is the penultimate week of Term 3. Today we have the deepest conversation of the year — using whakataukī, environmental language, arts, identity, and sophisticated vocabulary to talk about what truly matters.

> **A:** He aha te mea nui rawa atu ki a koe i tō ao?
> **B:** Ko tōku whānau, ko tōku reo, ko tōku taiao — ko ērā ngā [pou](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=pou) o tōku ora.
> **A:** He aha tō moemoeā mō Aotearoa?
> **B:** Ko tōku moemoeā: ka [toitū](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=toit%C5%AB) te reo Māori, ka toitū hoki te taiao — ka ora tātou katoa.
> **A:** He aha tō whakataukī pai rawa atu?
> **B:** "Ehara tāku toa i te toa takitahi, he toa takitini." Ko tā tēnei mōhio ko te [mana](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=mana) o ngā tāngata katoa.
> **A:** Ka wani kē! He aha tāu e tūmanako ana mō ō uri?
> **B:** Kei te tūmanako au ka kōrero pai rātou i te reo Māori, ka [tiaki](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=tiaki) hoki rātou i tēnei whenua ātaahua.

New sophisticated vocabulary:
**ngā pou o tōku ora** — the pillars of my life (pou = pillar)
**ka toitū** — will endure / remain sustainable
**Ka toitū te reo, ka toitū te taiao.** — When the language endures, the environment endures.
**mana o ngā tāngata katoa** — the dignity/authority of all people
**tūmanako** — hope / expectation
**He aha tāu e tūmanako ana mō ō uri?**
What do you hope for your descendants?
**Ka kōrero pai rātou** — they will speak well
**tēnei whenua ātaahua** — this beautiful land

*Tip: Ko ērā ngā pou o tōku ora — those are the pillars of my life. Pou appears again — the pillar that supports everything. Whānau, reo, taiao — three pillars. What are the three pillars of YOUR life? Can you say them in te reo?*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Deep conversation**

Build the deepest te reo conversation of the year — about what truly matters.

- I ask: He aha te mea nui rawa atu ki a koe i tō ao? Answer with Ko [X], ko [Y], ko [Z] — ngā pou o tōku ora.
- I ask: He aha tō moemoeā mō Aotearoa? Answer with Ka toitū + what you hope will endure.
- I ask: He aha tō whakataukī pai rawa atu? Say the whakataukī and explain its meaning.
- I ask: He aha tāu e tūmanako ana mō ō uri? Answer with Kei te tūmanako au ka [hope].

_Discussion prompt: He aha tāu e tūmanako ana mō ō uri? What do you hope for your descendants? You are Year 5–6 students — your descendants are decades away. But you are also somebody's descendant right now. What do you think the people who came before you hoped for you? Have those hopes been realised?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Ngā pou o tōku ora**

Write about the pillars of your life and your hopes.

- Write Ko [X], ko [Y], ko [Z] — ngā pou o tōku ora.
- Write your moemoeā for Aotearoa: Ko tōku moemoeā: ka toitū…
- Write your hope for your uri: Kei te tūmanako au ka [hope] ō mātou uri.
- ✦ Bonus: Write a full reflection (8 sentences) on what matters most — your pillars, your dreams, a whakataukī that guides you, your hope for the future. This is your Term 3 statement of identity. Make it true.

#### Friday: Aromatawai — Quiz & Review


**Monday questions**

- "Ehara tāku toa i te toa takitahi, he toa takitini." What does this whakataukī mean?
  - Strength comes from working alone 
  - My strength is not from one — it is the strength of many together ✅
  - Competition makes us strong 
  - The strongest person wins 
- What is the "rito" in the harakeke whakataukī?
  - The outer leaves of the flax 
  - The ground where the flax grows 
  - The heart shoot of the flax — representing the child/core of the family ✅
  - The bellbird 

**Tuesday questions**

- What does "whakamātau" mean?
  - to observe 
  - to research 
  - to discover 
  - to experiment / to test ✅
- "Ka kitea e mātou ko te [finding]." What grammatical feature is notable here?
  - Ka for future tense 
  - Passive voice — the discovery came to us ✅
  - E…ana for ongoing action 
  - Ko for identity 

**Wednesday questions**

- "Ka puta mai te āniwaniwa ina tutaki ana te rā me te ua." What does ina…ana mean?
  - because of 
  - although 
  - when / whenever (conditional) ✅
  - if 
- What does "wehewehe" mean?
  - to meet / encounter 
  - to emerge 
  - to separate / divide ✅
  - to observe 

**Thursday questions**

- "Ko ērā ngā pou o tōku ora." What does this sentence mean?
  - Those are the problems in my life 
  - Those are the pillars of my life ✅
  - Those are my children 
  - Those are my dreams 
- What does "tūmanako" mean?
  - fear / worry 
  - challenge 
  - hope / expectation ✅
  - memory

### Week 9

#### Monday: Arotake — Term 3 vocabulary and structure review

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — All Term 3 AOs consolidated · Grammar Levels 1–2 complete*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.3 — Term 3 review; AO 2.5 — All vocabulary

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Three terms of te reo Māori**

This is the second-to-last week of Term 3 and of your formal learning year. Today we celebrate everything Term 3 has added to your te reo Māori world — and look at how far three terms of daily practice has taken you.

> Term 3 has added these domains to your toolkit:
> 
> **BODY AND HEALTH:** māhunga, kanohi, ringa, waewae, mamae, kaimahi hauora, me okioki
> **TENSE MASTERY:** Kua (completed), E…ana (progressive), hiahia ki te, māku e, Ka taea e au te
> **IDENTITY AND COMMUNITY:** pepeha (full), marae, pōhiri, hongi, whaikōrero, uri, tīpuna
> **SCHOOL AND LEARNING:** akoranga subjects, evaluative language, taunakitanga
> **ARTS AND CULTURE:** toi whakairo, raranga, haka, waiata, tohunga
> **ENVIRONMENT:** kaitiakitanga, toitū, mauri, Ka ora te taiao ka ora tātou
> **WISDOM:** whakataukī, tikanga, tapu, noa, mana
> **SCIENCE:** whakamātau, rangahau, kitenga

The growth across three terms:

Term 1: "Kei te haere au ki te kura."
Term 2: "I te Rāhina, i haere mātou tokowhā ki te kura…"
Term 3: "Ko au tētahi kaitiaki o tēnei taiao — e ako ana au i te reo Māori hei tiaki i tōku ao, hei mōhio ki ngā mōrearea, hei tupu tōku ngākau i ngā whakataukī o ōku tīpuna."

Translation: "I am a guardian of this natural world — I am learning te reo Māori to protect my world, to understand the challenges, to grow my heart through the proverbs of my ancestors."

That sentence uses: Ko identity, E…ana progressive, hei (purpose), ngā tīpuna (ancestors), whakataukī, ngākau (heart), and multiple subordinate connections. You can write this. You are writing this.

*Tip: Read that Term 3 sentence aloud. Slowly. Own every word of it. You earned it.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Term 3 blitz**

Category rapid fire — cover the whole term.

- BODY: From māhunga to waewae — name ten body parts in order, head to toe.
- WHAKATAUKĪ: Who can say all four whakataukī from Week 8 from memory?
- ARTS: Name all the toi forms — toi whakairo, toi raranga, toi puoro… aim for 8.
- ENVIRONMENT: Ka ora te taiao, ka ora tātou — what does it mean and when would you use it?

_Discussion prompt: You have been learning te reo Māori for three terms — about 600 minutes of class time. In that time you have built a vocabulary of over 400 words and mastered 15+ grammar structures. What would you have thought at the start of Term 1 if someone told you this? What does it tell you about what daily practice can achieve?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Three-term audit**

A personal vocabulary and structure audit across all three terms.

- Write down every te reo word you can remember in 5 minutes — all three terms.
- Write one sentence using each of the six tense markers: Kei te, I, Ka, Kua, E…ana, Kāore i.
- Write your favourite whakataukī and explain what it means to you.
- ✦ Bonus: Write the most complex te reo sentence you have ever written. Draw on everything from all three terms. Make it say something true about who you are and what you value.

#### Tuesday: Arotake — Grammar deep review

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — All grammar structures Terms 1–3 consolidated*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.4 — Tense mastery; AO 2.3 — Grammar consolidation

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — The complete grammar picture**

Today we put the entire three-term grammar picture on the table. This is the complete toolkit — every structure you have learned, organised clearly so you can see how they connect.

> **IDENTITY: Ko** — Ko [name/thing] tōku [relationship].
> **DESCRIPTION: He** — He [adjective/noun] tēnei. He [adj] ake tērā.
> **PRESENT: Kei te** — Kei te [verb] [person] i [place].
> **ONGOING: E…ana** — E [verb] ana [person] i tēnei wā.
> **PAST: I** — I [verb] [person] i [time/place].
> **NARRATIVE: Ka** — Ka [verb] ia. Ka [verb] anō ia.
> **PERFECT: Kua** — Kua [verb] ia. Kua mutu.
> **NEGATIVES: Kāore** — Kāore [person] e [verb] ana. / Kāore [person] i [verb].
> **WANT: Hiahia** — Kei te hiahia [person] ki te [verb].
> **WILL: Māku e** — Māku/Māna e [verb].
> **ABLE: Ka taea** — Ka taea e [person] te [verb].
> **RULES: Me/Kaua** — Me [verb] tātou. Kaua e [verb].
> **COMPLEX: Ahakoa/Mehemea/I te wā/Nō te mea**

The test of grammar mastery is not reciting rules — it is fluent use.

A truly fluent sentence from a Term 3 student:

"Ahakoa i uaua ki a au i te tīmatanga, kua mārama ake au ināianei — ka taea e au te kōrero i te reo Māori, ka taea e au te tuhituhi hoki, ā ko tōku [moemoeā](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=moemoe%C4%81): ka kōrero tonu au i ēnei kupu tapu mō ōku uri."

Although it was difficult for me at the beginning, I now understand better — I am able to speak te reo Māori, I am able to write too, and my dream is: I will continue to speak these sacred words for my descendants.

Ahakoa (complex), Kua (perfect), Ka taea (ability), Ko + moemoeā (dream), Ka tonu (future ongoing). Every structure in one sentence.

*Tip: Write the test sentence above in your own words. Change the details to make it true for YOU. That is grammar mastery — not knowing the names, but being able to SAY it.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Grammar challenge**

Build sentences matching each structure one by one.

- Ko + He + Kei te in one passage — three sentences about yourself right now.
- I + Ka + Kua in sequence — three moments from this morning.
- Kāore e…ana + Kāore i + mehemea — three negative/conditional sentences.
- Ahakoa + nō te mea + nō reira — one complex sentence with all three.

_Discussion prompt: Look at that test sentence: ahakoa, kua, Ka taea, ko, moemoeā, ka tonu, uri. Seven different structures in one sentence. At the start of Term 1, how many of those words did you know? Now? What is the relationship between vocabulary and grammar — can you have one without the other?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Grammar self-test**

Test yourself on every major structure from three terms.

- Write a sentence using each of: Ko, He, Kei te, E…ana, I, Ka, Kua.
- Write a sentence using: Kāore e…ana, Kāore i, hiahia ki te, māku e, Ka taea.
- Write a sentence using: ahakoa, nō te mea, mehemea, i te wā.
- ✦ Bonus: Write a ten-sentence passage that uses at least fifteen different grammar structures correctly. This is your three-term grammar showcase.

#### Wednesday: Hanga rerenga — Showcase sentences: three terms

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — All AOs Terms 1–3 · Full sentence type showcase*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.3 — Showcase; AO 2.5 — Full vocabulary & expression

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Your best te reo ever**

Today you write the best sentences of your three-term te reo journey. There is no new content. Just you, your toolkit, and the task of producing your finest te reo Māori.

> Student showcase examples from across the year:
> 
> **"Ko te reo Māori ko tōku ara ki ngā tīpuna — he ara māmā, he ara aroha."**
> (Te reo Māori is my path to the ancestors — a light path, a loving path.)
> 
> **"I te wā e ako ana au i tēnei reo tapu, ka mārama aku karu ki tētahi ao hou — ko te ao Māori, ko te ao ātaahua."**
> (While I was learning this sacred language, my eyes opened to a new world — the Māori world, the beautiful world.)
> 
> **"Ka toitū te reo, ka toitū te tangata, ka toitū hoki te taiao — ko tērā tōku whakapono, ko tērā hoki tōku moemoeā."**
> (When language endures, people endure, and the natural world endures — that is my belief, and that is also my dream.)

What makes these sentences exceptional:

→ They are PERSONAL — these could not have been written by anyone else
→ They use MULTIPLE STRUCTURES seamlessly
→ They say something the writer DEEPLY BELIEVES
→ They use FIGURATIVE LANGUAGE (ara = path, literal AND metaphorical)
→ They CONNECT three terms of learning (tīpuna, tapu, mauri, moemoeā, whakapono)

Your showcase sentence should:
→ Use at least three different structures
→ Say something genuinely true about you
→ Include at least one word or phrase from each term
→ Feel like te reo Māori, not like an English sentence translated

*Tip: The best te reo sentence you can write today is the one that is most true. Not the most complex, not the longest — the most true. Write it in te reo Māori.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Best sentences together**

Class writes one final group showcase sentence — everyone contributes one element.

- Opening: a Ko statement about what te reo Māori is to us.
- Process: what happens when we learn it — E…ana, Ka, Kua.
- Result: what it gives us — He [noun/quality] tēnei.
- Closing: our hope for the future — Ko tērā tōku/ō mātou moemoeā.

_Discussion prompt: Ka toitū te reo, ka toitū te tangata, ka toitū hoki te taiao. Three things endure together. If language endures, people endure. If people endure, the natural world is protected. Three things, one survival. Is this true? Is it possible for a language to disappear without its people also losing something essential?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Your showcase sentences**

Write your three finest te reo Māori sentences of the year.

- Write your best SIMPLE sentence — true and precise.
- Write your best COMPLEX sentence — multiple structures, deep meaning.
- Write your SHOWCASE SENTENCE — the single finest te reo Māori sentence you have ever produced.
- ✦ Bonus: Write your "three-term testament" — three sentences, one from each term's perspective: who you were (Term 1), who you are becoming (Term 2), and who you intend to be (Term 3). Each sentence should use at least three structures from that term.

#### Thursday: Kōrero — Preparing for the Term 3 assessment

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — All AOs 1.1–2.5 · All six language modes*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 1.1 — Greetings; AO 1.2 — Introductions; AO 2.3 — Full conversation; AO 2.5 — Sophisticated vocabulary

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — The Term 3 standard**

Next week is your end-of-term assessment. Today we see what the Term 3 standard looks like — a conversation that draws on all three terms, uses sophisticated vocabulary and structures, and sounds like genuine te reo Māori.

> **A:** Tēnā koe. Ko wai tōu ingoa? He aha tō pepeha?
> **B:** Ko [name] tōku ingoa. Ko [maunga] tōku maunga, ko [awa] tōku awa, nō [place] ahau.
> **A:** He aha ngā mea nui rawa atu ki a koe i tō ao?
> **B:** Ko ngā pou o tōku ora ko tōku whānau, ko tōku reo, ko tōku taiao. E ako ana au i te reo Māori hei tiaki i ēnei taonga.
> **A:** Kua tae ki a koe tēnei mōhiotanga?
> **B:** Āe, kua mārama ake au i ia wiki, i ia wiki. Ka taea e au te kōrero i ngā hanganga katoa ināianei.
> **A:** He aha tōu whakataukī e tūāhuatia ana i a koe?
> **B:** "Ehara tāku toa i te toa takitahi, he toa takitini." Ko tā tēnei mōhio ko te mana o te mahi ngātahi.
> **A:** Ka wani kē! Tēnā koe, tēnā koe, tēnā koe.
> **B:** Tēnā koe, tēnā koe, tēnā koe.

What this conversation demonstrates:
✓ AO 1.1: Tēnā koe + three-part close
✓ AO 1.2: Full introduction with Ko + pepeha
✓ AO 1.4: Pepeha (maunga, awa, origin)
✓ AO 2.3: Extended sophisticated kōrero
✓ AO 2.5: Pou, taonga, mōhiotanga, whakataukī
✓ Grammar: Ko, E…ana, Kua, Ka taea, whakataukī explanation

New phrase:
**He aha tōu whakataukī e tūāhuatia ana i a koe?**
What proverb characterises / represents you?
**e tūāhuatia ana** — that characterises / represents (passive E…ana)

*Tip: He aha tōu whakataukī e tūāhuatia ana i a koe? — What proverb represents you? This is a beautiful question. Before the assessment, choose YOUR whakataukī. The one that speaks to who you are. Be ready to say it and explain it.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Assessment preparation**

Full practice conversation — using the Term 3 standard as your guide.

- Greet formally + full pepeha — all nine lines.
- He aha ngā mea nui rawa atu ki a koe i tō ao? Answer with Ko ngā pou o tōku ora ko…
- State your learning progress: Kua mārama ake au. Ka taea e au te…
- Share your whakataukī + explanation + three-part close.

_Discussion prompt: He aha tōu whakataukī e tūāhuatia ana i a koe? Which whakataukī represents you? "He toa takitini" (strength of many) — "Whāia te iti kahurangi" (pursue excellence) — "He tangata, he tangata, he tangata" (it is people). Before you answer, sit with it. The right whakataukī should feel like it has always been yours._

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Assessment preparation**

Prepare for the Term 3 end-of-term assessment.

- Write and memorise your full pepeha — all nine lines.
- Write Ko ngā pou o tōku ora ko… (three pillars of your life).
- Choose your whakataukī — write it and its explanation: Ko tā tēnei mōhio ko te…
- ✦ Bonus: Write the full conversation you plan to have in the assessment (12 lines minimum). Practise it until you can say it without looking. Then say it to a family member tonight.

### Week 10

#### Monday: Arotake — End-of-term vocabulary celebration

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — All AOs Terms 1–3 · Complete vocabulary review*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 1.1 — Greetings; AO 2.5 — All vocabulary; AO 2.3 — All structures

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Three terms: a vocabulary celebration**

Today we celebrate. In three terms of daily warm-up practice, you have built something remarkable. Let us count it, celebrate it, and prepare to show it.

> Three terms of te reo Māori. Here is your vocabulary toolkit:
> 
> TERM 1: Greetings · Ko · He · Kei te · Numbers 1–10 · food · places · wants · feelings · adjectives · conjunctions · adverbs · prepositions
> 
> TERM 2: I (past) · Ka (narrative) · Kua (perfect) · Kāore (negative) · All 16 pronouns · Numbers 1–100 · Dates/times/months · Colours · Comparatives · Weather · Seasons · Emotions · Character · Arts
> 
> TERM 3: Body · E…ana · Kua · hiahia · māku e · Ka taea · Pepeha (full) · Marae · Haka · Whakataukī · Kaitiakitanga · Science vocabulary · Formal/informal register · Argument and persuasion

What you can now DO:

✓ Have a sophisticated 10-minute conversation entirely in te reo Māori
✓ Write a full pepeha and deliver it from memory
✓ Explain a whakataukī and its deeper meaning
✓ Write a persuasive argument using taunakitanga
✓ Describe past, present, and future events fluently
✓ Express emotions, opinions, goals, and dreams
✓ Talk about the environment, the arts, the body, and the community
✓ Switch between formal and casual registers
✓ Use all 16 pronouns correctly
✓ Count to 100, say the time, and give the date
✓ Understand and produce complex sentences with subordinate clauses

This is real. You did this. Ka tino pai!

*Tip: Ka tino pai! — genuinely, truly excellent. Not just "good" — tino good. You have earned tino.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Three-term blitz**

The fastest, most comprehensive vocabulary review of the year.

- PEPEHA: One student delivers their full pepeha from memory. Class listens and checks.
- WHAKATAUKĪ: Who can say all four from Term 3 Week 8? Class helps where needed.
- NUMBERS: Count from 1 to 100 as a class in te reo — everyone joins in.
- SHOWCASE: Each student says their best single te reo sentence of the year. No repeats.

_Discussion prompt: Your best single te reo sentence of the year. Not the longest. Not the most complicated. The most TRUE. What do you want to say, in te reo Māori, about your year of learning? Say it. Own it._

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Final vocabulary audit**

Your last vocabulary audit of the year.

- Write every te reo word you know in 5 minutes. Count them.
- Write what you know today that you did not know at the start of Term 1.
- Write your three best te reo sentences of the year.

#### Tuesday: Arotake — Grammar final review

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — All grammar structures Terms 1–3*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.4 — Tense mastery; AO 2.3 — Grammar review

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Every structure, one final time**

Today we review every grammar structure from three terms — one final time. This is not about memorising rules. It is about confirming what you already know.

> Every structure in one passage:
> 
> **Ko ahau ko [name]. He ākonga au i tēnei kura.** (Ko · He)
> **Kei te ako tonu au i te reo Māori — e tipu ana tōku māramatanga i ia rā, i ia rā.** (Kei te · E…ana)
> **I tīmata au i tērā tau — kāore au i mōhio ki ētahi kupu.** (I · Kāore i)
> **Ka haere au ki te kura. Ka ako au. Ka hari tōku ngākau.** (Ka sequence)
> **Kua mārama ake au — kua tipu tōku reo.** (Kua)
> **Ka taea e au te kōrero i te reo Māori, ka taea e au te tuhituhi hoki.** (Ka taea)
> **Ahakoa i uaua, i mahi tonu au nō te mea ko tōku [moemoeā](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=moemoe%C4%81): ka kōrero tōku whānau i te reo Māori.** (Ahakoa · Nō te mea · Ko)

Count the structures in that passage:
1. Ko (identity)
2. He (description)
3. Kei te (present)
4. E…ana (progressive)
5. I (past)
6. Kāore i (past negative)
7. Ka (narrative × 3)
8. Kua (perfect × 2)
9. Ka taea (ability × 2)
10. Ahakoa (complex concession)
11. Nō te mea (reason)
12. Ko + moemoeā (dream statement)

Twelve structures in seven sentences. You know all of them.

*Tip: You know all of these. You have used them all. Today's review is not about learning — it is about confidence. Read the passage aloud. Feel how natural it sounds. That is YOUR voice in te reo Māori.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Structure identification**

Read the I Do passage aloud together. Identify every structure as a class.

- Identify: Ko, He, Kei te, E…ana — point to each one as you read.
- Identify: I, Kāore i, Ka (three times), Kua (twice).
- Identify: Ka taea (twice), Ahakoa, Nō te mea, Ko + moemoeā.
- Now write your own passage — same structures, your own words and story.

_Discussion prompt: Read your own three-term passage aloud. This is the grammar of your story — your Ko, your He, your Kua, your Ka taea. Every structure you know is in service of what YOU want to say. Grammar is not the goal — it is the tool. What story do you want to tell with these tools?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Grammar showcase**

Write a passage that uses every major structure from three terms.

- Write three sentences using Ko, He, and Kei te.
- Write three sentences using I, Ka, and Kua.
- Write three sentences using Ka taea, ahakoa, and nō te mea.
- ✦ Bonus: Combine all nine sentences into one flowing passage. Add connective words to make it flow. This is your three-term grammar showcase.

#### Wednesday: Hanga rerenga — Three-term portfolio sentence

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — All AOs · All sentence types Terms 1–3*

**Learning Objectives:** AO 2.3 — Portfolio showcase; AO 2.5 — Full expression

##### I Do

**Tāua Kōrero — Your portfolio sentence**

Today you write THE sentence — your single finest piece of te reo Māori from three terms of learning. This sentence will go in your portfolio, on the wall, and in your memory. It should say who you are, what matters to you, and what you carry into the future.

> Portfolio sentences from imagined students across the year:
> 
> **"Ka toitū te reo o ōku tīpuna i roto i āku kupu, i roto i āku mahi, i roto i āku moemoeā."**
> (The language of my ancestors endures within my words, within my actions, within my dreams.)
> 
> **"He ākonga noa iho ahau — engari, ko tōku ngākau e hiahia ana ki te tiaki i tēnei [taonga](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=taonga) ātaahua, ko te reo Māori."**
> (I am just a learner — but my heart desires to protect this beautiful treasure, te reo Māori.)
> 
> **"I tīmata au i tētahi kupu — ināianei, kua tipu ōku rerenga ki tētahi ao hou, ko te ao Māori."**
> (I started with one word — now, my sentences have grown into a new world, the Māori world.)

What makes a portfolio sentence:

→ It is PERSONAL — only you could have written it
→ It is TRUE — you believe every word
→ It CONNECTS to something bigger than yourself (reo, taiao, tīpuna, uri)
→ It uses MULTIPLE STRUCTURES seamlessly
→ It SOUNDS BEAUTIFUL — read it aloud and feel it

The three examples above each use:
"Ka toitū" — enduring / the perfect aspect
"Ko tōku ngākau e hiahia ana" — heart progressive want
"Kua tipu" — has grown (perfect) into a new world

Your portfolio sentence is not a homework task. It is a declaration.

*Tip: He ākonga noa iho ahau — I am just a learner. And yet — ko tōku ngākau e hiahia ana ki te tiaki i tēnei taonga. The heart wants to protect it. Humility + passion = the perfect learner of te reo Māori.*

##### We Do

**Kia Tūhono — Draft together**

Class writes one group portfolio sentence — and each student writes their own.

- Class sentence: Ko mātou ngā ākonga o tēnei akomanga — ka tipu tō mātou reo i ia rā, i ia rā.
- Now each student writes their own first draft — alone, in silence, in te reo.
- Share with a partner — read aloud, give feedback, improve.
- Final version: write it clearly, read it to the class.

_Discussion prompt: I tīmata au i tētahi kupu — I started with one word. What was your first word in te reo Māori? Kia ora? Āe? Your name in Ko? How many words do you know now? What does that journey — from one word to hundreds — feel like to look back on?_

##### You Do

**Māu Anō — Portfolio sentence**

Write your portfolio sentence.

- Draft: write three possible portfolio sentences — different approaches, different feelings.
- Choose: which one is most true? Which one sounds most like you in te reo?
- Final: write your chosen sentence carefully. This is your keepsake.
- ✦ Bonus: Write a portfolio paragraph (5 sentences) — your portfolio sentence as the opening, followed by four sentences that expand on it. What does this sentence say about who you are, what you have learned, and where you are going?

#### Thursday: Kōrero — He whakamātautau — End of Term 3 assessment

*Te Aho Arataki Marau — All AOs 1.1–2.5 · All six language modes assessed*

This is your final spoken assessment for Term 3. It is also the most sophisticated conversation you will have had in te reo Māori. Here is what the full Term 3 standard looks like:

> **A:** Tēnā koe. Ko wai koe? He aha tō pepeha?
> **B:** [Full nine-line pepeha from memory]
> **A:** Ka pai tō pepeha! He aha ngā pou o tōu ora?
> **B:** Ko ngā pou o tōku ora ko tōku whānau, ko tōku reo, ko tōku taiao — ko ērā ngā mea e tiaki ana i a au.

> **A:** He aha tōu moemoeā mō tō reo Māori?
> **B:** Ko tōku moemoeā: ka kōrero tōku whānau i te reo Māori, ka toitū hoki tēnei reo [tapu](https://maoridictionary.co.nz/search?keywords=tapu) mō ōku uri.
> **A:** Ka wani kē tāu kōrero! Tēnā koe, tēnā koe, tēnā koe.
> **B:** Tēnā koe, tēnā koe, tēnā koe.

Assessment checklist:
✓ AO 1.1: Tēnā koe + Tēnā koe ×3 close
✓ AO 1.2: Full pepeha with Ko sentences
✓ AO 1.4: Maunga, awa, iwi, hapū, marae, kura, ingoa, origin
✓ AO 2.3: Extended sophisticated conversation
✓ AO 2.5: Pou, moemoeā, whakataukī, whakapono, uri, toitū
✓ Grammar: Ko ×9 (pepeha), E…ana, Ka, Kua, ahakoa (embedded)

*Tip: Deliver your pepeha with pride. It carries your mountain, your river, your people, and your name. There is no more important thing you can say in te reo Māori.*

- Whakataukī + explanation — say it, explain both levels of meaning.

_Discussion prompt: Ko tōku moemoeā: ka kōrero tōku whānau i te reo Māori. Is this your dream too? Or is it something else? Before your assessment, sit quietly for one minute and answer: what do YOU hope for your te reo Māori — not for the assessment, not for the teacher, but for yourself, for your life?_
